Like, they’ll respond or there’ll be an opening to set something up, and I just don’t do the next step.\n\nSo it’s not like I can’t get out of bed or take care of basic things, but my actual life, the parts I care about and was trying to build momentum on, feel like they’ve slowed way down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, not in a clear way. Like, I haven’t been sitting there thinking about hurting myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like I get this tired, blank feeling, where I’m kind of worn down by the day before it even starts, but I don’t think it turns into “I should do something to myself.”\n\nThe “life isn’t worth living” part is harder to answer, because sometimes when I’m really low I’ll have these vague thoughts like, “What is the point of all this?” or “I don’t know how to keep doing this same empty-feeling day,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually wanting to die. It feels more like I want everything to stop being so heavy for a while.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts of hurting myself. Mostly I just feel stuck and drained.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms start after a stressful life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it “stressful” in the sense of something bad happening, exactly, but it did start after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule.\n\nThat change kind of shifted the whole rhythm at home, and after that was when this current low stretch started. It’s been about three weeks now. At first I thought I was just adjusting to the new routine, but it kept going, so… yeah, it does feel like it started after that life change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I’m realizing I’m not totally sure where to draw the line between “another period” and this same thing kind of coming and going.\n\nLooking back, it feels like this depressed, slowed-down version of me has been around off and on for maybe about three months now. Not every single day at the same intensity, and there were times where I could sort of push through it or convince myself it was just a mood or being tired or whatever. But the pattern has been there longer than I first wanted to admit.\n\nThe last few weeks are when it’s gotten more obvious, I guess. Like that’s when I really started noticing, “Okay, this isn’t just a random off day.” But if I’m being honest, the low, stuck feeling had been hovering in the background before that. So… yes, I’ve had stretches like this, or at least this current stretch has been sort of lingering off and on for around three months, with the past few weeks being the clearest.\n\nDoctor: During the past few weeks, have you still felt down on days when your wife being at work wasn’t really on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. It’s not only when I’m actively thinking, “Oh, my wife is at work and I’m alone,” or anything that direct.\n\nMost days lately, I kind of wake up already with this heavy, flat feeling. Like before I’ve even really had a chance to think through what the day is or what she’s doing, it’s just… there. The apartment feels too quiet, even if that’s not exactly what I’m focusing on. And I’ll try to do something that usually makes the day feel more like mine, like picking up my guitar for a bit, but even that doesn’t really lift me much. It’s like the sound is happening, but I’m not connecting to it the way I normally would.\n\nSo, yes, I guess the change in routine may have started it or made it more noticeable, but the down feeling doesn’t seem to depend on me sitting there thinking about her being gone. It’s just kind of there on its own most days.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch… I’m not sure I can point to a totally separate one that was clearly its own thing for two weeks or more.\n\nWhat I can say is, looking back, this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for maybe about three months. It hasn’t been equally bad the whole time, and I think that’s part of why I didn’t immediately register it as “a period” of depression. It would ease up a little, or I’d tell myself I was just tired or in a funk, and then it would kind of come back.\n\nThe past few weeks are where it’s felt the most noticeable and harder to brush off. So I don’t know if the answer is yes in the sense of a separate episode before this, but this whole low stretch has definitely been going on in some form for longer than just the last couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but lately it has felt kind of close to that some mornings.\n\nMost days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s like the apartment is too quiet, and everything feels kind of still in a bad way. I’ll try to do something normal, like pick up my guitar for a bit, because usually that makes me feel more like myself, but lately it doesn’t really do much. It’s not that I’m sitting there thinking some dramatic thought the whole time, like “nothing will ever get better,” but it feels hard to imagine feeling different when I’m in it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe not hopeless in a constant, absolute way, but there’s definitely been this feeling like I’m stuck under something and I can’t quite see how I’m supposed to get out of it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve been feeling kind of heavy and slow, but I don’t know if I’d say “unusually tired” in the sense of, like, physically exhausted all the time.\n\nSome days I do feel like it takes more effort to get myself moving, but I haven’t really been tracking my energy separately, if that makes sense. It all sort of blends together with the mood, so I’m not sure whether I’m actually low on energy or just… not feeling much drive to do things. I guess I’ve noticed I’m not as quick to start stuff, but I don’t know if that’s tiredness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep’s been… not great. I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night staring at the ceiling, but it’s broken up in this really annoying way.\n\nI’ll usually fall asleep at some point, but then I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just kind of switches on. It’s almost always something stupidly practical, like bills I haven’t dealt with yet, or some track I started and never finished, or just this list of things I’m behind on. And once that starts, I’m not fully awake in a useful way, but I’m not really asleep either.\n\nThen by the time it’s actually morning, I already feel worn out, like the day started before I was ready for it. So I guess the change is less about not sleeping at all and more that the sleep doesn’t feel solid or restful.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. That’s actually been really noticeable, and it’s frustrating because it happens with stuff I normally like doing.\n\nLike if I sit down to work on some code, I’ll read the same line over and over and it just doesn’t seem to… land. I know the words are there, I know I should be able to follow the logic, but I keep losing the thread and then I realize I’ve been staring at it for ten minutes without making a decision. And then I start second-guessing whether the problem is actually hard or whether my brain just isn’t catching.\n\nIt’s similar with music. I’ll try to arrange something, and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out what should come next, and normally I can at least feel my way through it. Lately it’s like every option feels blank or wrong, so I just loop it and loop it and don’t actually move forward.\n\nSo yeah, concentrating has been harder. Not completely impossible, but enough that simple things take way longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way where someone would look at me and immediately think something is wrong, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nLike I’ll sit with my laptop open for ages before I even really do anything. I’ll have the code or whatever in front of me, and I know the next step is simple, like just put my hands on the keyboard, plug something in, type the first line, but there’s this weird delay between deciding to do it and actually moving. It’s like my body is buffering or something.\n\nSpeaking-wise, I’m not sure if other people would notice, but I feel like I take longer to answer. I pause more, and I kind of have to push the words out instead of them just coming normally. So yeah, I’d say slower, but more like I’m moving through thick air than like I’m completely shut down.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest, like I don’t suddenly hate the things I usually like or anything. It’s more… I don’t know, maybe I have a harder time getting into them?\n\nLike with music, I still care about it in theory. If someone asked me, “Do you want to be someone who makes music?” I’d say yes, definitely. But actually sitting down and feeling that spark or getting absorbed in it has been less reliable. Sometimes I’ll open something and just sort of look at it, and I can’t tell if I’m uninterested or if I’m just not getting any momentum.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say it’s gone exactly. Pleasure is maybe muted some days, but I don’t know if that counts as “lost interest.” It’s not like nothing matters to me. It’s just harder to feel pulled toward things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight or about your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not because of weight or body shape. It’s not like I’m looking in the mirror and thinking I need to be smaller or anything like that.\n\nThe reason I brought up eating is more that, for the past few weeks, it’s just been… off. I’ll skip breakfast, and then somehow lunch disappears too, not really on purpose. It’s more like I don’t get around to it, or food just doesn’t seem worth the effort in the moment. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just kind of pick at it instead of eating a normal amount.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me think, okay, maybe this isn’t just one weird day here and there. But no, it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or worried about gaining it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really strong to me. I’m not walking around thinking I’m a terrible person or that I don’t deserve to exist or anything like that.\n\nGuilt is harder, because I do second-guess myself a lot. Like I’ll think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m probably letting people down,” and then I can get stuck going over that in my head. But I don’t know if that’s excessive guilt in the way you mean it. It’s not like I believe I did some awful thing. It’s more this vague, heavy feeling that I’m not keeping up or not being who I should be.\n\nSo… maybe some guilt, but I’m not sure I’d call it extreme. And worthless, not really.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have no appetite at all,” but compared to normal, I’m definitely not getting hungry in the same way.

What made me notice it is that for the last few weeks, my eating has just been kind of off. I’ll skip breakfast, and it’s not usually some deliberate choice—I just don’t get around to it, or nothing sounds good enough to bother with. Then lunch will kind of disappear too, and by the time I realize it, it’s already late in the day.

And then even at dinner, like if my wife cooked something, I’ll sit down and I know I should eat, and I’ll maybe have some, but I end up picking at it more than eating a normal amount. It feels less like I’m trying not to eat and more like the hunger signal is quieter, or food just doesn’t feel worth the effort. My jeans feeling looser is what made me think, okay, maybe this has actually been happening more than I was admitting.

So I don’t know if the answer is yes in the sense of a separate episode before this, but this whole low stretch has definitely been going on in some form for longer than just the last couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but lately it has felt kind of close to that some mornings.\n\nMost days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s like the apartment is too quiet, and everything feels kind of still in a bad way. I’ll try to do something normal, like pick up my guitar for a bit, because usually that makes me feel more like myself, but lately it doesn’t really do much. It’s not that I’m sitting there thinking some dramatic thought the whole time, like “nothing will ever get better,” but it feels hard to imagine feeling different when I’m in it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe not hopeless in a constant, absolute way, but there’s definitely been this feeling like I’m stuck under something and I can’t quite see how I’m supposed to get out of it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve been feeling kind of heavy and slow, but I don’t know if I’d say “unusually tired” in the sense of, like, physically exhausted all the time.\n\nSome days I do feel like it takes more effort to get myself moving, but I haven’t really been tracking my energy separately, if that makes sense. Pleasure is maybe muted some days, but I don’t know if that counts as “lost interest.” It’s not like nothing matters to me. I mean, it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have no appetite at all,” but compared to normal, I’m definitely not getting hungry in the same way.\n\nWhat made me notice it is that for the last few weeks, my eating has just been kind of off. I’ll skip breakfast, and it’s not usually some deliberate choice—I just don’t get around to it, or nothing sounds good enough to bother with. Then lunch will kind of disappear too, and by the time I realize it, it’s already late in the day.\n\nAnd then even at dinner, like if my wife cooked something, I’ll sit down and I know I should eat, and I’ll maybe have some, but I end up picking at it more than eating a normal amount. It feels less like I’m trying not to eat and more like the hunger signal is quieter, or food just doesn’t feel worth the effort. My jeans feeling looser is what made me think, okay, maybe this has actually been happening more than I was admitting.\n\n[Last Question]\nDuring this period, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 31 / no_occupation / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet and even picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think I have, most days lately. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic like that, so sometimes I almost talk myself out of calling it “depressed,” but there’s been this heavy, flat feeling when I wake up.

Like the apartment feels too quiet, even if nothing is actually wrong in that moment. I’ll be sitting there in the morning and it’s like everything has this dullness over it. And even things that normally make me feel a little more like myself—like picking up my guitar—don’t really lift it much. I’ll hold it or play a little, and it just doesn’t land the way it usually does.

So, yeah, I guess the answer is yes. Most days, there’s been this low, heavy feeling hanging around.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, hi. Um… I guess what finally made me come in is my eating has been kind of off for the past few weeks. It’s not like I’m deliberately trying to skip meals—I just don’t really think about food, or it sounds like too much effort. I feel bad about that because she went to the trouble, and then I’m sitting there acting like I’m not grateful or something.\n\nAnd my jeans have started feeling loose, which is what made it feel less like “oh, I’m just having a weird week” and more like maybe I should talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s been about three weeks now. At first I thought I was just adjusting to the new routine, but it kept going, so… yeah, it does feel like it started after that life change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I’m realizing I’m not totally sure where to draw the line between “another period” and this same thing kind of coming and going.\n\nLooking back, it feels like this depressed, slowed-down version of me has been around off and on for maybe about three months now. Not every single day at the same intensity, and there were times where I could sort of push through it or convince myself it was just a mood or being tired or whatever. But the pattern has been there longer than I first wanted to admit.\n\nThe last few weeks are when it’s gotten more obvious, I guess. Like that’s when I really started noticing, “Okay, this isn’t just a random off day.” But if I’m being honest, the low, stuck feeling had been hovering in the background before that. It hasn’t been equally bad the whole time, and I think that’s part of why I didn’t immediately register it as “a period” of depression. It would ease up a little, or I’d tell myself I was just tired or in a funk, and then it would kind of come back.\n\nThe past few weeks are where it’s felt the most noticeable and harder to brush off. I mean, I’ve been feeling kind of heavy and slow, but I don’t know if I’d say “unusually tired” in the sense of, like, physically exhausted all the time.\n\nSome days I do feel like it takes more effort to get myself moving, but I haven’t really been tracking my energy separately, if that makes sense. It all sort of blends together with the mood, so I’m not sure whether I’m actually low on energy or just… not feeling much drive to do things. I guess I’ve noticed I’m not as quick to start stuff, but I don’t know if that’s tiredness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep’s been… not great. I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night staring at the ceiling, but it’s broken up in this really annoying way.\n\nI’ll usually fall asleep at some point, but then I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just kind of switches on. It’s almost always something stupidly practical, like bills I haven’t dealt with yet, or some track I started and never finished, or just this list of things I’m behind on. And once that starts, I’m not fully awake in a useful way, but I’m not really asleep either.\n\nThen by the time it’s actually morning, I already feel worn out, like the day started before I was ready for it. And then I start second-guessing whether the problem is actually hard or whether my brain just isn’t catching.\n\nIt’s similar with music. I’ll try to arrange something, and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out what should come next, and normally I can at least feel my way through it. Lately it’s like every option feels blank or wrong, so I just loop it and loop it and don’t actually move forward.\n\nSo yeah, concentrating has been harder. Not completely impossible, but enough that simple things take way longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way where someone would look at me and immediately think something is wrong, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nLike I’ll sit with my laptop open for ages before I even really do anything. I’ll have the code or whatever in front of me, and I know the next step is simple, like just put my hands on the keyboard, plug something in, type the first line, but there’s this weird delay between deciding to do it and actually moving. It’s like my body is buffering or something.\n\nSpeaking-wise, I’m not sure if other people would notice, but I feel like I take longer to answer. I pause more, and I kind of have to push the words out instead of them just coming normally. So yeah, I’d say slower, but more like I’m moving through thick air than like I’m completely shut down.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest, like I don’t suddenly hate the things I usually like or anything. It’s more… I don’t know, maybe I have a harder time getting into them?\n\nLike with music, I still care about it in theory. If someone asked me, “Do you want to be someone who makes music?” I’d say yes, definitely. But actually sitting down and feeling that spark or getting absorbed in it has been less reliable. Sometimes I’ll open something and just sort of look at it, and I can’t tell if I’m uninterested or if I’m just not getting any momentum.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say it’s gone exactly. Pleasure is maybe muted some days, but I don’t know if that counts as “lost interest.” It’s not like nothing matters to me. It’s just harder to feel pulled toward things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight or about your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not because of weight or body shape. It’s not like I’m looking in the mirror and thinking I need to be smaller or anything like that.\n\nThe reason I brought up eating is more that, for the past few weeks, it’s just been… off. I’ll skip breakfast, and then somehow lunch disappears too, not really on purpose. It’s more like I don’t get around to it, or food just doesn’t seem worth the effort in the moment. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just kind of pick at it instead of eating a normal amount.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me think, okay, maybe this isn’t just one weird day here and there. But no, it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or worried about gaining it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really strong to me. I’m not walking around thinking I’m a terrible person or that I don’t deserve to exist or anything like that.\n\nGuilt is harder, because I do second-guess myself a lot. But I don’t know if that’s excessive guilt in the way you mean it. It’s not like I believe I did some awful thing. It hasn’t been equally bad the whole time, and I think that’s part of why I didn’t immediately register it as “a period” of depression. It would ease up a little, or I’d tell myself I was just tired or in a funk, and then it would kind of come back.\n\nThe past few weeks are where it’s felt the most noticeable and harder to brush off. So I don’t know if the answer is yes in the sense of a separate episode before this, but this whole low stretch has definitely been going on in some form for longer than just the last couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but lately it has felt kind of close to that some mornings.\n\nMost days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s like the apartment is too quiet, and everything feels kind of still in a bad way. I’ll try to do something normal, like pick up my guitar for a bit, because usually that makes me feel more like myself, but lately it doesn’t really do much. It’s not that I’m sitting there thinking some dramatic thought the whole time, like “nothing will ever get better,” but it feels hard to imagine feeling different when I’m in it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe not hopeless in a constant, absolute way, but there’s definitely been this feeling like I’m stuck under something and I can’t quite see how I’m supposed to get out of it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve been feeling kind of heavy and slow, but I don’t know if I’d say “unusually tired” in the sense of, like, physically exhausted all the time.\n\nSome days I do feel like it takes more effort to get myself moving, but I haven’t really been tracking my energy separately, if that makes sense. It all sort of blends together with the mood, so I’m not sure whether I’m actually low on energy or just… not feeling much drive to do things. I guess I’ve noticed I’m not as quick to start stuff, but I don’t know if that’s tiredness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep’s been… not great. I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night staring at the ceiling, but it’s broken up in this really annoying way.\n\nI’ll usually fall asleep at some point, but then I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just kind of switches on. It’s almost always something stupidly practical, like bills I haven’t dealt with yet, or some track I started and never finished, or just this list of things I’m behind on. And once that starts, I’m not fully awake in a useful way, but I’m not really asleep either.\n\nThen by the time it’s actually morning, I already feel worn out, like the day started before I was ready for it. So I guess the change is less about not sleeping at all and more that the sleep doesn’t feel solid or restful.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. That’s actually been really noticeable, and it’s frustrating because it happens with stuff I normally like doing.\n\nLike if I sit down to work on some code, I’ll read the same line over and over and it just doesn’t seem to… land. I know the words are there, I know I should be able to follow the logic, but I keep losing the thread and then I realize I’ve been staring at it for ten minutes without making a decision. And then I start second-guessing whether the problem is actually hard or whether my brain just isn’t catching.\n\nIt’s similar with music. I’ll try to arrange something, and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out what should come next, and normally I can at least feel my way through it. Lately it’s like every option feels blank or wrong, so I just loop it and loop it and don’t actually move forward.\n\nSo yeah, concentrating has been harder. Not completely impossible, but enough that simple things take way longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way where someone would look at me and immediately think something is wrong, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nLike I’ll sit with my laptop open for ages before I even really do anything. I’ll have the code or whatever in front of me, and I know the next step is simple, like just put my hands on the keyboard, plug something in, type the first line, but there’s this weird delay between deciding to do it and actually moving. It’s like my body is buffering or something.\n\nSpeaking-wise, I’m not sure if other people would notice, but I feel like I take longer to answer. I pause more, and I kind of have to push the words out instead of them just coming normally. So yeah, I’d say slower, but more like I’m moving through thick air than like I’m completely shut down.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest, like I don’t suddenly hate the things I usually like or anything. It’s more… I don’t know, maybe I have a harder time getting into them?\n\nLike with music, I still care about it in theory. If someone asked me, “Do you want to be someone who makes music?” I’d say yes, definitely. But actually sitting down and feeling that spark or getting absorbed in it has been less reliable. Sometimes I’ll open something and just sort of look at it, and I can’t tell if I’m uninterested or if I’m just not getting any momentum.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say it’s gone exactly. Pleasure is maybe muted some days, but I don’t know if that counts as “lost interest.” It’s not like nothing matters to me. It’s just harder to feel pulled toward things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight or about your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not because of weight or body shape. It’s not like I’m looking in the mirror and thinking I need to be smaller or anything like that.\n\nThe reason I brought up eating is more that, for the past few weeks, it’s just been… off. I’ll skip breakfast, and then somehow lunch disappears too, not really on purpose. It’s more like I don’t get around to it, or food just doesn’t seem worth the effort in the moment. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just kind of pick at it instead of eating a normal amount.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me think, okay, maybe this isn’t just one weird day here and there. But no, it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or worried about gaining it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really strong to me. I’m not walking around thinking I’m a terrible person or that I don’t deserve to exist or anything like that.\n\nGuilt is harder, because I do second-guess myself a lot. Like I’ll think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m probably letting people down,” and then I can get stuck going over that in my head. But I don’t know if that’s excessive guilt in the way you mean it. It’s not like I believe I did some awful thing. It’s more this vague, heavy feeling that I’m not keeping up or not being who I should be.\n\nSo… maybe some guilt, but I’m not sure I’d call it extreme. And worthless, not really.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you actually felt less hungry than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have no appetite at all,” but compared to normal, I’m definitely not getting hungry in the same way.\n\nWhat made me notice it is that for the last few weeks, my eating has just been kind of off. I’ll skip breakfast, and it’s not usually some deliberate choice—I just don’t get around to it, or nothing sounds good enough to bother with. Then lunch will kind of disappear too, and by the time I realize it, it’s already late in the day.\n\nAnd then even at dinner, like if my wife cooked something, I’ll sit down and I know I should eat, and I’ll maybe have some, but I end up picking at it more than eating a normal amount. It feels less like I’m trying not to eat and more like the hunger signal is quieter, or food just doesn’t feel worth the effort. My jeans feeling looser is what made me think, okay, maybe this has actually been happening more than I was admitting.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, most days lately. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic like that, so sometimes I almost talk myself out of calling it “depressed,” but there’s been this heavy, flat feeling when I wake up.\n\nLike the apartment feels too quiet, even if nothing is actually wrong in that moment. I’ll be sitting there in the morning and it’s like everything has this dullness over it. And even things that normally make me feel a little more like myself—like picking up my guitar—don’t really lift it much. I’ll hold it or play a little, and it just doesn’t land the way it usually does.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess the answer is yes. Most days, there’s been this low, heavy feeling hanging around.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s not like I’m deliberately trying to skip meals—I just don’t really think about food, or it sounds like too much effort. It wasn’t like one dramatic morning where I woke up and everything was suddenly bad. It was more like it crept in after her schedule changed, and then after a bit I realized, okay, this isn’t just a couple off days.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function in your usual day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I mean, I’m not totally falling apart or anything. I’m still doing the basic stuff around the house—dishes get done eventually, laundry doesn’t pile up too insanely, I can get myself through the day. So in that sense I keep telling myself, like, “Okay, you’re functioning.”\n\nBut the bigger stuff has kind of stalled out. I had a few music projects I’d been trying to move forward, just little things I was excited about before, and now they’re just sitting there. I’ll open a file or look at my notes and then just sort of… stop. And I’d reached out to a couple musicians about maybe playing together or working on something, and I haven’t really followed through. Like, they’ll respond or there’ll be an opening to set something up, and I just don’t do the next step.\n\nSo it’s not like I can’t get out of bed or take care of basic things, but my actual life, the parts I care about and was trying to build momentum on, feel like they’ve slowed way down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, not in a clear way. Like, I haven’t been sitting there thinking about hurting myself, if that’s what you mean. It’s more like I get this tired, blank feeling, where I’m kind of worn down by the day before it even starts, but I don’t think it turns into “I should do something to myself.”\n\nThe “life isn’t worth living” part is harder to answer, because sometimes when I’m really low I’ll have these vague thoughts like, “What is the point of all this?” or “I don’t know how to keep doing this same empty-feeling day,” but it doesn’t feel like I’m actually wanting to die. It feels more like I want everything to stop being so heavy for a while.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve had thoughts of hurting myself. Mostly I just feel stuck and drained.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms start after a stressful life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I don’t know if I’d call it “stressful” in the sense of something bad happening, exactly, but it did start after my wife got settled into her full-time work schedule.\n\nThat change kind of shifted the whole rhythm at home, and after that was when this current low stretch started. It’s been about three weeks now. At first I thought I was just adjusting to the new routine, but it kept going, so… yeah, it does feel like it started after that life change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had another period in your life when you felt depressed like this?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I’m realizing I’m not totally sure where to draw the line between “another period” and this same thing kind of coming and going.\n\nLooking back, it feels like this depressed, slowed-down version of me has been around off and on for maybe about three months now. Not every single day at the same intensity, and there were times where I could sort of push through it or convince myself it was just a mood or being tired or whatever. But the pattern has been there longer than I first wanted to admit.\n\nThe last few weeks are when it’s gotten more obvious, I guess. Like that’s when I really started noticing, “Okay, this isn’t just a random off day.” But if I’m being honest, the low, stuck feeling had been hovering in the background before that. So… yes, I’ve had stretches like this, or at least this current stretch has been sort of lingering off and on for around three months, with the past few weeks being the clearest.\n\nDoctor: During the past few weeks, have you still felt down on days when your wife being at work wasn’t really on your mind?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I think so. It’s not only when I’m actively thinking, “Oh, my wife is at work and I’m alone,” or anything that direct.\n\nMost days lately, I kind of wake up already with this heavy, flat feeling. Like before I’ve even really had a chance to think through what the day is or what she’s doing, it’s just… there. The apartment feels too quiet, even if that’s not exactly what I’m focusing on. And I’ll try to do something that usually makes the day feel more like mine, like picking up my guitar for a bit, but even that doesn’t really lift me much. It’s like the sound is happening, but I’m not connecting to it the way I normally would.\n\nSo, yes, I guess the change in routine may have started it or made it more noticeable, but the down feeling doesn’t seem to depend on me sitting there thinking about her being gone. It’s just kind of there on its own most days.\n\nDoctor: Before this current stretch, have you ever had a separate period in your life when you felt depressed like this for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch… I’m not sure I can point to a totally separate one that was clearly its own thing for two weeks or more.\n\nWhat I can say is, looking back, this depressed, slowed-down kind of pattern has been hanging around off and on for maybe about three months. It hasn’t been equally bad the whole time, and I think that’s part of why I didn’t immediately register it as “a period” of depression. It would ease up a little, or I’d tell myself I was just tired or in a funk, and then it would kind of come back.\n\nThe past few weeks are where it’s felt the most noticeable and harder to brush off. So I don’t know if the answer is yes in the sense of a separate episode before this, but this whole low stretch has definitely been going on in some form for longer than just the last couple weeks.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly every time, but lately it has felt kind of close to that some mornings.\n\nMost days I wake up and there’s just this heavy, flat feeling already there, before anything has even happened. It’s like the apartment is too quiet, and everything feels kind of still in a bad way. I’ll try to do something normal, like pick up my guitar for a bit, because usually that makes me feel more like myself, but lately it doesn’t really do much. It’s not that I’m sitting there thinking some dramatic thought the whole time, like “nothing will ever get better,” but it feels hard to imagine feeling different when I’m in it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe not hopeless in a constant, absolute way, but there’s definitely been this feeling like I’m stuck under something and I can’t quite see how I’m supposed to get out of it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve been feeling kind of heavy and slow, but I don’t know if I’d say “unusually tired” in the sense of, like, physically exhausted all the time.\n\nSome days I do feel like it takes more effort to get myself moving, but I haven’t really been tracking my energy separately, if that makes sense. It all sort of blends together with the mood, so I’m not sure whether I’m actually low on energy or just… not feeling much drive to do things. I guess I’ve noticed I’m not as quick to start stuff, but I don’t know if that’s tiredness exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep’s been… not great. I am sleeping, so it’s not like I’m up all night staring at the ceiling, but it’s broken up in this really annoying way.\n\nI’ll usually fall asleep at some point, but then I wake up around four or five in the morning, and my brain just kind of switches on. It’s almost always something stupidly practical, like bills I haven’t dealt with yet, or some track I started and never finished, or just this list of things I’m behind on. And once that starts, I’m not fully awake in a useful way, but I’m not really asleep either.\n\nThen by the time it’s actually morning, I already feel worn out, like the day started before I was ready for it. So I guess the change is less about not sleeping at all and more that the sleep doesn’t feel solid or restful.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. That’s actually been really noticeable, and it’s frustrating because it happens with stuff I normally like doing.\n\nLike if I sit down to work on some code, I’ll read the same line over and over and it just doesn’t seem to… land. I know the words are there, I know I should be able to follow the logic, but I keep losing the thread and then I realize I’ve been staring at it for ten minutes without making a decision. And then I start second-guessing whether the problem is actually hard or whether my brain just isn’t catching.\n\nIt’s similar with music. I’ll try to arrange something, and I’ll replay the same eight bars again and again, trying to figure out what should come next, and normally I can at least feel my way through it. Lately it’s like every option feels blank or wrong, so I just loop it and loop it and don’t actually move forward.\n\nSo yeah, concentrating has been harder. Not completely impossible, but enough that simple things take way longer than they should.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably slower than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not in a dramatic way where someone would look at me and immediately think something is wrong, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nLike I’ll sit with my laptop open for ages before I even really do anything. I’ll have the code or whatever in front of me, and I know the next step is simple, like just put my hands on the keyboard, plug something in, type the first line, but there’s this weird delay between deciding to do it and actually moving. It’s like my body is buffering or something.\n\nSpeaking-wise, I’m not sure if other people would notice, but I feel like I take longer to answer. I pause more, and I kind of have to push the words out instead of them just coming normally. So yeah, I’d say slower, but more like I’m moving through thick air than like I’m completely shut down.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I don’t think I’ve completely lost interest, like I don’t suddenly hate the things I usually like or anything. It’s more… I don’t know, maybe I have a harder time getting into them?\n\nLike with music, I still care about it in theory. If someone asked me, “Do you want to be someone who makes music?” I’d say yes, definitely. But actually sitting down and feeling that spark or getting absorbed in it has been less reliable. Sometimes I’ll open something and just sort of look at it, and I can’t tell if I’m uninterested or if I’m just not getting any momentum.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say it’s gone exactly. Pleasure is maybe muted some days, but I don’t know if that counts as “lost interest.” It’s not like nothing matters to me. It’s just harder to feel pulled toward things the way I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight or about your body shape?\n\nPatient: No, not because of weight or body shape. It’s not like I’m looking in the mirror and thinking I need to be smaller or anything like that.\n\nThe reason I brought up eating is more that, for the past few weeks, it’s just been… off. I’ll skip breakfast, and then somehow lunch disappears too, not really on purpose. It’s more like I don’t get around to it, or food just doesn’t seem worth the effort in the moment. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just kind of pick at it instead of eating a normal amount.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me think, okay, maybe this isn’t just one weird day here and there. But no, it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or worried about gaining it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really strong to me. I’m not walking around thinking I’m a terrible person or that I don’t deserve to exist or anything like that.\n\nGuilt is harder, because I do second-guess myself a lot. Like I’ll think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m probably letting people down,” and then I can get stuck going over that in my head. But I don’t know if that’s excessive guilt in the way you mean it. It’s not like I believe I did some awful thing. It’s more this vague, heavy feeling that I’m not keeping up or not being who I should be.\n\nSo… maybe some guilt, but I’m not sure I’d call it extreme. And worthless, not really.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you actually felt less hungry than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have no appetite at all,” but compared to normal, I’m definitely not getting hungry in the same way.\n\nWhat made me notice it is that for the last few weeks, my eating has just been kind of off. I’ll skip breakfast, and it’s not usually some deliberate choice—I just don’t get around to it, or nothing sounds good enough to bother with. Then lunch will kind of disappear too, and by the time I realize it, it’s already late in the day.\n\nAnd then even at dinner, like if my wife cooked something, I’ll sit down and I know I should eat, and I’ll maybe have some, but I end up picking at it more than eating a normal amount. It feels less like I’m trying not to eat and more like the hunger signal is quieter, or food just doesn’t feel worth the effort. My jeans feeling looser is what made me think, okay, maybe this has actually been happening more than I was admitting.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, most days lately. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic like that, so sometimes I almost talk myself out of calling it “depressed,” but there’s been this heavy, flat feeling when I wake up.\n\nLike the apartment feels too quiet, even if nothing is actually wrong in that moment. I’ll be sitting there in the morning and it’s like everything has this dullness over it. I’ll hold it or play a little, and it just doesn’t land the way it usually does.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess the answer is yes. It’s not like I’m looking in the mirror and thinking I need to be smaller or anything like that.\n\nThe reason I brought up eating is more that, for the past few weeks, it’s just been… off. I’ll skip breakfast, and then somehow lunch disappears too, not really on purpose. It’s more like I don’t get around to it, or food just doesn’t seem worth the effort in the moment. Then by dinner, even if my wife cooked something, I’ll just kind of pick at it instead of eating a normal amount.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me think, okay, maybe this isn’t just one weird day here and there. But no, it’s not because I’m trying to lose weight or worried about gaining it.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t say “worthless,” exactly. That word feels really strong to me. I’m not walking around thinking I’m a terrible person or that I don’t deserve to exist or anything like that.\n\nGuilt is harder, because I do second-guess myself a lot. Like I’ll think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m probably letting people down,” and then I can get stuck going over that in my head. But I don’t know if that’s excessive guilt in the way you mean it. It’s not like I believe I did some awful thing. It’s more this vague, heavy feeling that I’m not keeping up or not being who I should be.\n\nSo… maybe some guilt, but I’m not sure I’d call it extreme. And worthless, not really.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you actually felt less hungry than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I wake up and think, “I have no appetite at all,” but compared to normal, I’m definitely not getting hungry in the same way.\n\nWhat made me notice it is that for the last few weeks, my eating has just been kind of off. I’ll skip breakfast, and it’s not usually some deliberate choice—I just don’t get around to it, or nothing sounds good enough to bother with. Then lunch will kind of disappear too, and by the time I realize it, it’s already late in the day.\n\nAnd then even at dinner, like if my wife cooked something, I’ll sit down and I know I should eat, and I’ll maybe have some, but I end up picking at it more than eating a normal amount. It feels less like I’m trying not to eat and more like the hunger signal is quieter, or food just doesn’t feel worth the effort. My jeans feeling looser is what made me think, okay, maybe this has actually been happening more than I was admitting.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, most days lately. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything dramatic like that, so sometimes I almost talk myself out of calling it “depressed,” but there’s been this heavy, flat feeling when I wake up.\n\nLike the apartment feels too quiet, even if nothing is actually wrong in that moment. I’ll be sitting there in the morning and it’s like everything has this dullness over it. And even things that normally make me feel a little more like myself—like picking up my guitar—don’t really lift it much. I’ll hold it or play a little, and it just doesn’t land the way it usually does.\n\nSo, yeah, I guess the answer is yes. Most days, there’s been this low, heavy feeling hanging around.\n\nDoctor: Have you actually lost weight during this time?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I haven’t weighed myself a bunch or tracked it carefully, so I can’t give you an exact number, but my jeans are starting to feel loose, and that’s usually how I notice something like that.\n\nIt kind of fits with how I’ve been eating, too. Like I said, I’ll skip breakfast without really deciding to, and then lunch just sort of gets away from me. Then by dinner, even if my wife made something and I’m sitting there knowing I should eat a normal meal, I’m mostly picking at it. So I think I probably have lost some weight over the last few weeks, just based on my clothes and how little I’ve been eating."
